# References

Learn more links for every stop in `docs/curriculum.md`: Sources, then the picks for each stop, best first.

Rules:

- Free to read: no paywall, no login, no paid books.
- Each link opens at the topic: a chapter, section or lecture page, a PDF at `#page=N`, or a paper about exactly that stop. `Part` says where to look.
- arXiv papers link to the abstract page (it offers HTML and PDF). Never a doi.org landing page or a site home page.
- University level or better: lecture notes, free textbooks, encyclopedias, peer-reviewed papers, standards. Official docs only for software stops.
- Right level: beginner stops get beginner reading.
- When the free arXiv copy has another title than the journal version, the Note says `arXiv title: "..."`.
- Never from memory. Ids and metadata come from `python3 scripts/check-ref.py <doi or arXiv id>`; every link was opened and its heading found.
- `python3 scripts/check-ref.py docs/references.md` checks the ids, titles, years and these rules. Add `--links` to open every link.

## Gaps

From the curators, November 2026. Stops with no pick: `dqi`, `network-milestones`, `atomic-clocks`, `quantum-standards`, `cuda-q`, `braket`, `who-builds`, `roadmaps`, `investment`, `national-programs`, `supply-chain`.

### Math, foundations, circuits

No stop is left empty. Weak spots:

- `multi-controlled`, `pauli-rotations`: only one section of a research paper. No free source at Intermediate level found.
- `two-qubit-gates`: only Krantz et al. (graduate review). It covers iSWAP, CZ and cross-resonance, not Mølmer-Sørensen or fSim.
- `topological-model`: only Nayak et al. (research review).
- `single-qubit-unitary`: only the OpenQASM 2 paper (U(θ,φ,λ) = Rz(φ)Ry(θ)Rz(λ)).
- `su2`: no free pick shows the minus sign after a 360 degree turn.
- `decomposition`: no free pick shows the split into two-level gates.
- `qtm`: no free pick shows that QTMs and circuits have the same power.
- One pick only: `groups`, `mach-zehnder`, `quantum-zeno`, `ghz-paradox`, `no-signaling`, `fredkin`, `zx-calculus`.

### Algorithms, theory

- `dqi`: no free source I could verify. The only full text is the open access Nature paper (blocked, see below). The Algorithm Zoo entry has no anchor.
- Thin (1 pick only): `analog-simulation`, `adapt-vqe`, `imaginary-time`, `tda`, `simon-attacks`, `speedup-needs-structure`, `forrelation`, `proofs-of-quantumness`, `verification`, `noise-limits`. Most have a stronger open access paper under "Could not verify".
- Level: these Intermediate stops have no pick at undergraduate level, only graduate notes, reviews or papers: `quantum-counting`, `amplitude-estimation`, `minimum-finding`, `graph-queries`, `analog-simulation`, `fermion-mapping`, `state-prep`, `parameter-shift`, `xeb`.

### Information, protocols, networks

- `network-milestones` (Beginner): `-`. No free beginner or undergraduate source covers DARPA 2003, Delft 2021 and Boston 2024. Closest: Azuma et al. 2023 review, Section V.H (graduate, ends before 2024). The research papers (pompili-2021-multinode, knaut-2024-memory) are not allowed on a Beginner stop.
- `atomic-clocks` (Beginner): `-`. The BIPM SI Brochure is blocked (see below). Only graduate picks exist: Ludlow et al. 2015, Section VII.A (arXiv:1407.3493v2, page 59) and Degen et al. 2017, Section III.D (page 6).
- `quantum-standards`: `-`. BIPM is blocked; von Klitzing 1980 and Josephson 1962 are not free.
- One pick only: `conference-key`, `quantum-imaging`.
- Scope facts the picks do not back: Jinan-1 12,900 km key (2025) in `satellites`; the 2019 and 2023 dates in `ligo`; flight trials 2024 to 2025 in `inertial-navigation`; NCSC and EU dates in `pqc-migration`; agency positions in `qkd-vs-pqc` (the picks give the QKD side); ITU-T Y.3800 and ISO/IEC 23837 in `qkd-standards`; HAWK withdrawal (July 2026) in `other-pqc`; draft FIPS 206 in `fn-dsa` (no draft found on csrc.nist.gov); the 2026 Pinnacle preprint in `q-day`; the Cloudflare traffic share in `hybrid-crypto`; NIST's CURBy beacon in `di-randomness`.

### Hardware, error correction, software, industry

No free, exact, reachable source (Picks row is `-`):

- `cuda-q`: only the NVIDIA docs fit, and that host is blocked.
- `braket`: only the AWS docs fit, and that host is blocked.
- `who-builds`, `roadmaps`, `investment`: only company pages, consulting reports or blocked government pages (DARPA, GAO, National Academies).
- `national-programs`: the laws and strategies sit on blocked government hosts (congress.gov, govinfo.gov, gov.uk, OECD, EU).
- `supply-chain`: no free official report found on a reachable host.

Partial or weak cover:

- `nobel-prizes`: only the 2022 Bell-test work (SEP). The 2012 and 2025 prizes need nobelprize.org, which is blocked.
- `geopolitics`: NIST IR 8105 covers the crypto risk only. The export rule (Federal Register 2024-19633) and OECD pages are blocked.
- `topological`: picks explain the Majorana route; no free source found for Majorana 1 (2025) or the 2026 critique.
- `ft-algorithm-demos`: one case study (Bluvstein 2023); the PRX (Nov 2026) source was not found.
- `cryogenics`, `spin-qubits`, `sdk-zoo`: no pick at Beginner level. Picks are a research paper (Krinner), a graduate review (Burkard) and software papers (Killoran, Smith).
- One pick only: `silicon-fab`, `tket`, `open-systems-sim`, `optimal-control`, `ft-algorithm-demos`, `nobel-prizes`, `geopolitics`.
- Many papers have an open journal copy on nature.com, journals.aps.org, iopscience, pnas.org or springeropen, all blocked here. Those picks link the arXiv PDF instead (for example Linke, Bonilla Ataides, Wu, Kubica, Breuckmann, Babbush, Nation, Takagi, Lee, Fox, Tindall, Krinner).
